No fifth core doc. *(This supersedes every older "two docs" / "three source docs" line below — those are archived history.)* **Money model of record → [`CASHFLOW-LAB.html`](./CASHFLOW-LAB.html)** (founder-locked 25 Jul, #556).

## ▶️ START HERE — the current state of the company *(26 Jul 2026 — supersedes every dated block below)*

**Locked by the founder, 26 Jul, in his words:** *"on 31 aug if this is not all done i stop."* That is the outside edge. Between now and then there is **one thing that decides whether we have a business: the product must be able to send from the client's own mailbox.** Everything else is already built or does not matter yet.

- **What we sell.** A **managed cold-outreach service** trading as **Milla&Vida** on the FIGSY engine. **Vida** = our operator console. **Milla** = the client portal (masked leads · 👍 approve / ✕ pass · concierge chat · meetings · reports). **Nexus** = each client's private brain. K.I.N.D remains the registered company.
- **The money, as it actually runs** (locked 24–25 Jul, #492/#541): one dollar wallet per client · first purchase **$99 = the onboarding pack, 100 approved leads included** · then a flat **$4 per approved lead, FINAL** · no expiry, no hold, no capture-on-booking, no release · a dead email is never charged · meetings are reported, not refunded · **only the client's 👍 ever spends — operators never**. Two gates protect it: **minimum 20 approvals** the first time round, and **30 days with no approvals suspends them** (they un-suspend on their own next approval, no operator in the loop).
- **The cost floor is ~$457/month, not ~$190** (#556 — the old figure omitted Smartlead ~$94, Instantly ~$37, Zoho ~$3 and the Anthropic runtime). Two consequences we now sell against: a client must approve **~13/month just to pay for their own inbox**, and the **$99 pack was −$52 in month one** — RETIRED figure, and the reason the price moved: at **$299** (3 Aug, #609) the pack clears **≈ +$165** in month one. **The repeat is the business; the pack is the door.**
- **🛑 THE BLOCKER, stated plainly: we cannot deliver a paying client.** `figsy.ts:26` is `const FROM = COLD_FROM` — one shared module constant for every client — `client_inboxes` is read by **no send path**, and **no SMTP client is installed**. So *"sends on your own warmed inbox"* is not built. That is **#211**, 🔴 since 30 Jun, now broken into **#547–#553** (LAUNCH-PAD Block A). Founder-locked 26 Jul: **Instantly is OURS, Smartlead is the CLIENTS'**, and both run **inside our own product** — *"we use our own product for us"*, no CSV hand-off.
- **What we CAN do today: demo.** MBF (#544) is one fixed demo environment, always — 40 invented people, no migration needed, and it physically cannot send. **The demo is sellable; delivery is not.** So the honest sales position is: sell the demo, take the deposit, deliver when Block A lands.
- **Why the docs were rewritten today.** Founder: *"the one source of truth is so wrong."* He was right. `BUILD-STATUS.md` — a fifth status doc, forbidden by `CLAUDE.md` — claimed *"the ONLY items not built: #515 + CI"* while the whole sending spine was 🔴. It is retired (#555). LAUNCH-PAD is now one table of items, owners and mirrored dots, ordered Blocks A→E, with everything that does not get us live moved out and its new home named.
- **The rule that comes out of this:** every claim in a doc is either **read from the code in the same session** or it is marked as a claim. The last three sessions' worth of "done" that turned out to be half-done all trace to the same habit — reporting memory as evidence.


### 💼 THE TWO-MODEL DECISION — Base + Advanced, one engine *(founder-locked 31 Jul; logged 2 Aug, #608)*

**Logged late, and deliberately so.** The founder locked this on 31 Jul and then held it back: *"once we through with all this log that we need to ensure opus verifies all docs are up to date and clean. Stale docs = errors."* The cleaning is #608; this is the thing it was gating.

**The founder's case, in his words:** *"I know from my own sales experience selling a 1 product model is not strong enough… I think we need to have 2 offerings min."* He also asked not to be agreed with reflexively — *"Do not just agree with me"* — and the position below survived that challenge rather than skipping it.

**THE DECISION: Base + Advanced. Two packages, ONE engine.** Not two products, not two codebases, not two prices for the same thing. The engine that finds, scores, writes, sends, triages and books is identical in both; what differs is how much of it a client is entitled to and how much of our attention comes with it.

**Why two beats one here, on this business's own numbers:**
- **One price cannot express two buyers.** The $299 pack + $4/approved model is a single dial *(written when the pack was $99; price re-locked 3 Aug — the argument is unchanged)*. A client approving 20 leads a month and one approving 100 are the same product to us and wildly different economics — with one package the second is under-served and the first is over-sold.
- **The repeat is the business.** ⚠️ **UPDATED 3 Aug:** this said the **$99 pack is −$52 in month one**, which was true and is exactly why the price moved. At **$299** month one clears **≈ +$165** and covers acquisition too. The repeat still carries the business, but the door no longer runs at a loss. `CASHFLOW-LAB` recovers if they come back. A second tier gives a reason to come back that is not "buy more of the same".
- **Advanced is sold before it is built** — deliberately. It is a commercial position, not a build order. Nothing in Advanced should be constructed until a client has said yes to it and the price has been tested against a real conversation.

**What is NOT decided, and must not be inferred:** the price points, the exact feature split, and whether Advanced is a higher included-lead count, a higher-touch service, or an intelligence layer. Sketch figures discussed on 31 Jul ($249 Starter / $499–599 Advanced / $10–12 per lead) were **brainstorming and are not locked** — the founder said so explicitly at the time. The lock is the SHAPE: two packages, one engine.

**Coaching stays exactly as it is.** The tabbed Coaching rebuild (#601, PR #1239) is **parked on founder hold** — not cancelled, not merged. Milla's existing Coaching page is what clients see, and coaching is **not** a paid add-on: it remains a value-add on a booked meeting, which is what makes it a retention lever rather than another thing to price.

## 🏆 CREDIBILITY RULE — nothing public goes live half-full (locked 13 Jun)
> **Founder principle, applies to EVERYTHING public-facing:** we never ship a surface that looks empty or half-built — an empty Watch page, a Drop with no video, social icons linking to bare profiles, a thin Prompt Library. **Half-full = "not a real company" = lost credibility.** So: **build it fully on the branch now, but flip it LIVE only when it's genuinely populated.** Build ≠ go-live. Every website item below is gated this way (orphan/unlinked until full). Same for socials (164): icons built, hidden until the profiles have real content.

## 🎬 WEBSITE RESOURCES — CONTENT-FIRST PLAN (locked 13 Jun)
> **The principle:** Claude builds every page, all the copy, and the *video slots* **now**. The founder records video **after** and pastes it in — adding a video is a **one-line paste of a YouTube ID**, never a code change. Get the content shells **built**; flip them **live only when full** (per the Credibility Rule above).

**🔑 HOW THE DROP WORKS (founder, 13 Jun — corrected):** The Drop is a **SERIES**, not one post. **Each drop showcases 3–5 products/features.** We have a LOT of product → that's **many drops**. Build them **now, WITHOUT video** (drops are content showcases, complete without video). The **Prompt Library is the one big multi-item gallery**. **Product Video / Watch page → HOLD, do not build yet.**

**The resources — BUILD vs HOLD (13 Jun):**
| # | Resource | Decision | What it is | Inventory |
|---|----------|----------|------------|-----------|
| 1 | **The Drop** | 🛠️ **BUILD NOW (no video)** | A **series** of drop posts, each showcasing **3–5 products**. Seed the archive with several drops grouped from the shipped product (the AI Family · the lead engine · the outreach engine · the command centre · the connected platform · built-for-Africa). Newest on top. No video needed. | 93 |
| 2 | **Watch (Product Video)** | ⏸️ **HOLD — not yet** | One comprehensive walkthrough. **Do NOT build now** (founder 13 Jun). Revisit when the founder is ready to film. | 93 + 163 |
| 3 | **Prompt Library** ⭐ | 🛠️ **BUILD NOW** | The big searchable/filterable gallery — ready-made prompts (Dept + Category filters, "Request a prompt", Copy). Seed richly. | 162 |
| 4 | **Resources nav** | ⏸️ after the above are full | Dropdown wiring across the site. Wire only once the resources behind it are populated (Credibility Rule). | 118 |

**Division of labour:**
- 🤖 **Claude builds now:** the **Drop series page** (`apps/website/the-drop.html`, several drops × 3–5 products, no video) + the **Prompt Library** (`apps/website/prompt-library.html`, seeded richly). Brand-matched to the marketing site. Built on the working branch; orphan/unlinked until launch (Credibility Rule).
- 🧍 **Founder later:** approve the drop groupings; (eventually) film the product video when ready — **not now**.

**Sequence (now):** ① build the Drop series (no video) · ② build the Prompt Library · ③ **HOLD** Watch/Product Video · ④ wire the Resources nav only once the live ones are full.

**Go-live timing:** content is **built now**, pages stay **orphan/unlinked until the 19 Jun launch** (Drop 01 = the launch itself), then go live with the Resources nav — exactly the orphan-until-wired model PR #503 already uses. Nothing of the portal launch gate changes.

**Drop cadence after launch:** each release (≈weekly, post-19) = **1 new Drop card** + **1 in-portal "What's New" entry** (R19, item 78). Claude drafts the copy; founder approves + records the optional clip.

## 🗓️ THE 3-MONTH PLAN (Jun → Sep) — where we're headed after launch
> The founder's-eye view of the next quarter. Item-level detail: `PRODUCT-INVENTORY.md` §3 + `V2-TRACKER.md`. Each phase is **gated** — don't start the next before its trigger.

### 📅 MONTH 1 — LAUNCH + CLEAR THE QUEUE (Fri 19 Jun → ~19 Jul)
**Goal: launch clean, onboard the first pilots, and ship the whole post-19 queue fast (velocity = the moat).**
- **Launch & GTM:** go live 19th · 10 warm outreach + LinkedIn 1/day · record real product demo + Drop 01 video + onboarding Looms · homepage proof block with real data · GTM funnel instrumentation.
- **Ship the locked design queue:** Alta-style **inbox rebuild** (112) · **agent "AI Family"** cards (125) · **sequence-builder** recolor (82) · **agent side-panel consistency** (113a) · the 25 R-release PRs · shell redesign.
- **Money & admin cleanup:** ⭐ **client invoicing** (136a — 🔒 locked 13 Jun: **USD billing · NO VAT until a financial benchmark · Stripe issues the receipt, we only pull & display it** in-portal) · **Partner Hub earnings ZAR → USD** · Flutterwave/2nd-processor decision.
- **Company engine hardening:** invite-email delivery · owner drill-down · manager role · per-rep routing/offboarding/calendars.
- **Onboarding & segmentation (174–177):** website→firmographics read · seat-based routing (1=self-serve / 2+=concierge) · 14-day company trial on bundled data · white-glove implementation (CRM + optional BYO-Apollo). Full plan: cashflow §13/§14.
- **First clients:** the multi-rep client + partner pipeline onboard; pilot feedback drives the next sprint.

### 📅 MONTH 2 — INTELLIGENCE LAYER (late Jul → Aug · GATED 10+ paying clients)
**Goal: turn the outcome data into a compounding advantage. Trigger: ~10+ paying clients producing real outcomes.**
- **The Learning Engine** (build order: Train-FIGSY RAG → evals → outcome feedback loop → contextual bandit → recall/memory → model routing). The African cross-client outcome data is the input no competitor can replicate.
- Intent signals · A/B → contextual bandit · morning-brief · ICP auto-refinement · conditional branching · waterfall enrichment live · adaptive send volume · Milla CRM pull.
- **Context-backed MCP server (#59)** · Product Hunt + G2 listings · ★ shared winning-play library (network-compounding).

### 📅 MONTH 3 — THE AGENT FAMILY + PRICING (Aug → Sep · GATED on margin data)
**Goal: expand the family and move toward outcome pricing. Trigger: proven unit margin ≥ ~28%.**
- **DENISE deep build (#54)** — auto-book · notetaker · objection handling · proposal-from-transcript · Vapi voice (she owns the phone).
- **LENA** (Customer Success agent) · **TONY** (Operations agent) · multi-agent orchestration.
- **Outcome pricing per meeting (#60)** (gated ≥28% margin) · built-in CRM Kanban · mobile app (PWA) · pan-African design partners.

**↔ Parallel/ongoing all quarter:** admin RLS refactor · deliverability monitoring · legal rings · key rotation · GTM content engine · per-country partner replication (Kenya + Ghana via partners).

- **🌍 AFRICA GTM — RESEARCH-HARDENED (10 Jun deep-research, ~40 sources; verdicts below are sourced, the tiering held):**
  - **Priority order confirmed: 1) SA · 2) Nigeria · 3) Kenya (fast-follow ~Q4'26) · 4) Ghana (opportunistic via NG partner) · 5) Egypt (defer indefinitely — Arabic-first, EF English "low" → FIGSY's English engine mismatched).** Evidence: SA = Africa's best English (EF #13 globally) + best card rails for recurring billing + most mature SaaS market; Nigeria = largest SMB pool + best-of-Africa Apollo coverage (with SA) + partner-led entry matches how Nigerian B2B actually buys; Kenya = #1 VC funding (~$934M '25) **but energy-skewed (weak sales-tool signal)**, thinner Apollo data, M-Pesa-first billing weak for card recurring.
  - **🟢 THE LANE IS OPEN:** **no African-built AI-SDR/cold-email competitor found**; global tools (Apollo/Instantly/Smartlead) have zero localized African GTM, pricing, payments, or POPIA/NDPR positioning. **Watch: Trembi** (East Africa — positioning as "Apollo alternative for Africa"). **Concrete wedge: Apollo itself doesn't accept African mobile-money/local payments — we do (Flutterwave).**
  - **Data ceiling CONFIRMED:** Apollo strongest (for Africa) in SA + Lagos enterprise/mid-market; **thin in Kenya/Ghana and for SMBs generally**; PDL fallback = breadth not depth. Proprietary African contact graph = the real moat (roadmap). Quantify empirically: the 4-country previewCount test.
  - **Nigeria billing note:** research rates **Paystack subscriptions** the best-fit for NG SaaS billing (bank-transfer/USSD culture, card declines high) — we have Flutterwave; evaluate Paystack as an NG-specific addition when the first NG client lands.
  - **Deliverability:** no evidence African-origin mail is penalised *when sent from reputable infra with SPF/DKIM/DMARC* (which we do); risk attaches to IP/domain reputation, not nationality. Action: seed-list placement tests into Gmail/Outlook ZA/NG/KE post-launch.
  - **Runway honesty (estimate):** at $29–99/seat, ~$10k MRR ≈ 100–300 seats ≈ 10–30 SMB clients ≈ plausibly **6–12 months post-launch** on trust-led African sales — **unless the partner channel produces multi-client batches → the partner motion is the highest-leverage GTM decision of the year** (pattern evidence: Flutterwave/Salesforce-Africa scaled via partners; Yoco shows direct works only where physically present → SA-direct + NG-via-Demmy is exactly right).
  - **🤝 PARTNER CHANNEL (the highest-leverage GTM lever) — partners on the books:** **#1 Demmy (Nigeria, live).** **#2 Ireland partner (added 16 Jun)** — small but **very well connected**; sells the **trade-playbook** motion (electricians · plumbers · HVAC · photographers · locksmiths). **Ireland is NOT a target market** — this is an *opportunistic, partner-led channel*, not a market entry. **Guardrails (founder, 16 Jun):** build **NOTHING** Ireland-specific · partner uses **BYO-Apollo / own data** (keeps the single-key §13 ToS exposure flat) · price **USD** · **GDPR** already covers the EU · **Africa stays the build priority** (don't let a well-connected "yes" bend the roadmap). Rationale: bounded downside (the partner does the selling), free upside (early developed-market revenue + case studies/logos + a proof the motion travels). **Sales asset: `docs/PARTNER-BRIEF.md`** (pitch · 5 trade playbooks · pricing · 20% recurring terms).
  - **🇺🇸 US VERDICT: US-LATER, CLEARLY (sourced):** ~110 AI-SDR vendors, $400M+ VC, median vendor revenue only ~$2.2M; 11x scandal (fake customers, ~$3M real vs ~$14M claimed ARR, "70–80% churn" per employee — TechCrunch Mar'25); US cold-email replies degraded to **~3.4% average ('26)**; Gmail/Yahoo 0.1% working spam ceiling; premature multi-market scaling = best-documented startup killer (Startup Genome ~70%). US arguments (USD revenue, data depth) are capturable WITHOUT US GTM: price in USD (we do), US-grade infra (we do). **Tripwires to revisit US (founder-gated):** (a) ~$10–20k MRR / 25+ paying clients across SA+NG, (b) >85% 6-month logo retention, (c) FIGSY African reply rates beating the 3.4% US average. Low-cost middle path then: serve African companies selling *into* the US.
  - *(Original directional tiering below, kept for the record:)*
  - **Tier 1 engines: 🇿🇦 South Africa** (best Apollo coverage — dogfood ICP proved ~72k · POPIA built = category-of-one compliance claim · English · cards work · home turf) **+ 🇳🇬 Nigeria** (largest SMB market · **partner Demmy already live** · Flutterwave solves payments · watch: thinner data, NDPR ⏸ until first NG client, sender-reputation care).
  - **Tier 2: 🇰🇪 Kenya** (East-Africa hub, fintech-adoption culture, ODPC ⏸ until first KE client) · **🇬🇭 Ghana** (easy English entry — partner-led, not HQ focus).
  - **Tier 3 (defer): 🇪🇬 Egypt / 🇲🇦 Morocco** (Arabic/French localisation we don't have) · 🇷🇼🇲🇺🇧🇼 (tiny volume, opportunistic).
  - **How we target:** ① dogfood — FIGSY sells K.I.N.D (warmup campaign IS this; scale it) · ② replicate the Demmy partner model per country (1 partner each KE+GH in month 1–2; trust-broker markets) · ③ lead with compliance as marketing (POPIA/NDPR = claims competitors can't make) · ④ open low per-seat pricing as the weapon vs $500+/mo US tools · ⑤ **before pushing any country, run `previewCount` on the canonical ICP — if Apollo returns thin, that market waits for the PDL/multi-source layer (now wired, key-gated).**
  - **The volume ceiling is DATA, not demand:** Apollo thins fast outside SA → runway to first 10–50 clients ≈ same-or-faster than a US launch (warm market, no AI-SDR noise, partner channel); runway to 500+ needs the multi-source/African-data moat. **Founder action: run the 4-country previewCount test (prompts provided 10 Jun) — the best free market-sizing data we can get.**

For a small team, VELOCITY is the moat** — the window between launch and the intelligence layer is where competitors close in. Plus from the deep-dive:
    - **Positioning line to steal verbatim: "level up your whole team to your top performer."**
    - **Two-audience framing:** the rep feels TIME saved; the **owner pays for VISIBILITY** (accurate pipeline/forecast without chasing reps) → sell to both; owner = budget holder = 2c command centre.
    - **Headline KPI/ROI: sell in TIME SAVED PER SEAT** (e.g. "~8h/rep/month") — surface it on the dashboard; it's the number the owner renews on.

>
> Cuts through the green ticks above. A lot of code shipped today; **almost none of it
> is deployed or verified in a real environment.** Read this before assuming we're "on track."

**The hard truths:**
1. **Nothing is deployed; nothing is end-to-end tested.** There is **no automated test suite** (no `test` script). All of D1–D5 (List-Unsubscribe, plain-text, cold-FROM), the warmup cap, P-a signer, and the admin fixes are **typecheck-clean only** — never run against a real DB, Resend, or inbox. **Typecheck ≠ working.** First real proof comes only at the Tue 9 deploy + Smoke Tests.
2. **P-a + CAL-min are backend-only → currently INERT.** No portal field exists to set `signer_name` / `booking_url`, so clients can't use them. "Done" = misleading; they're *backend-ready*, gated on UI + migration 012.
3. **Warmup cap has a blind spot.** It counts `figsy_sent_emails`, but the **day-1 batch path inserts NULL enrollment/campaign ids into NOT-NULL columns → those inserts fail → day-1 sends are never counted.** The cap reliably guards the *sequence* path only. (Pre-existing constraint bug — needs a fix or the cap to count from `outcome_events`.)
4. **`signer_name` is a latent landmine.** If the portal sends it before migration 012 runs, the `PATCH /me` upsert fails → **breaks ALL settings saves.** Migration 012 MUST run before any UI sends it.
5. **Admin still bypasses RLS.** The admin frontend uses the `service_role` key across ~13 pages — the biggest architectural security gap, **unfixed** (Week 1–2 refactor).
6. **Launch rests entirely on one un-rehearsed Tue 9 deploy** going perfectly: merge→main, run migrations 010 + 012, set/verify env vars (cold-FROM, admin key), nothing else breaks. No dry run yet.
7. **Smoke tests T1–T7 not started.** The real end-to-end verification hasn't begun. T1 (fresh signup) has never been run, ever.

**Fair risk read for Fri 19:** the *code* is in good shape and progressing fast; the **gap is verification + deploy choreography, not features.** Date is still plausible **only if** the Tue 9 deploy is clean AND Smoke Test 2 passes first try — neither is proven. Realistic buffer is thin: the day-1 cap fix, the admin RLS refactor, and any smoke-test failures all compete for the same 2–3 days. If the deploy slips or smoke tests find real breaks, **Mon 22 is the honest fallback.**

**Most valuable next moves (in order):** (1) do a **dry-run deploy to a staging/throwaway** to de-risk Tue 9; (2) **run Smoke Test T1 fresh-signup** ASAP — it's never been tested; (3) fix the day-1 cap blind spot; (4) build the P-a/CAL-min portal fields *with* migration 012 so they're actually usable.

### 🎯 V2 MONTH-END PLAN — LIVE BY 30 JUN (locked 8 Jun by founder)
> Founder set the target + chose the scope: **"Per-rep + company OS."** This is THE core that must be in production by 30 Jun. It is a *scoped* V2, not all 18 sections — forcing everything would build the half-baked system we refuse to ship.
>
> **🚦 GATE (day 1, non-negotiable):** V1 in prod is NOT verified yet — warmup/T8 deliverability never tested. We do **not** build per-rep on an unverified base. **9am: Step 5 (first FIGSY warmup campaign) + T8 (deliverability), then T1–T10.** Foundation must be green before staging build starts.
>
> **IN by 30 Jun (the durable money engine):**
> - **#88 per-rep** — each rep their own autonomous FIGSY (identity · calendar · booking · leads scoped to the member, not the workspace).
> - **Owner Command Centre** (V2-9) — one company dashboard over all reps.
> - **Seats** + **Usage & Budget** (request → approve/deny, hybrid seat+usage) — one company payment, N FIGSYs. *(Founder: "vital.")*
> - **Performance funnel** (§17) — per-rep + company rollup (per-rep is meaningless without the rollup).
>
> **SLIPS to July (designed, not month-end — layer ON the per-rep foundation):** full branching sequence builder (#89) · multi-channel LinkedIn/WhatsApp/SMS · Train-FIGSY (§18) · Integrations Hub (#84) · §16 Smart Inbox rich taxonomy · the V2 cosmetic redesign items (V2-1/2/3/4/6/8/10/11/12).
>
> **DATED CRITICAL PATH (22 days):**
> - **Wk0 · Mon 8 – Fri 12 — VERIFY + STABILISE.** 9am smoke gate (Step 5 + T8 → T1–T10). Warmup ramping. First client onboarded on shared model as pilot. Partner solid. **Finalise #88 schema design + stand up staging (hard prereq).**
> - **Wk1 · Mon 15 – Fri 19 — PER-REP FOUNDATION (staging).** Slice 1 per-rep identity (calendar/booking/signer/leads → `client_members`). Slice 2 per-rep ownership (campaigns/sent-emails owned + meeting routing to the right rep's calendar). Migrations written + dry-run.
> - **Wk2 · Mon 22 – Fri 26 — COMPANY OS (staging).** Slice 3 seat billing (per-seat wallets + company budget + request/approve usage). Slice 4 Owner Command Centre + Performance funnel rollup. End-to-end integrate on staging.
> - **Wk3 · Mon 29 – Tue 30 — HARDEN + SHIP.** Migration dry-run on a staging clone, full per-rep smoke (new T11 per-rep flow), deploy to prod **30 Jun**. Mon 29 is the buffer for slippage.
>
> **Risks:** (1) the smoke gate finds real deliverability/prod breakage → eats Wk0, pushes everything right; (2) the workspace→member migration on a live client's data is the single riskiest step — staging dry-run is mandatory; (3) scope creep back toward "everything" — hold the line, July layers the rest.

- **12 Aug, second entry (🔧 EVERYTHING THE WALK FOUND, FIXED — and the fix for #383 turned out to be a live send-path defect, not bookkeeping):** the founder ordered every finding fixed rather than logged. **#383 WAS REAL AND IT WAS SHIPPING.** `20260710_increment_emails_sent.sql` has existed since 10 Jul and was **never added to `PENDING_MIGRATIONS`, the only list that executes (O3)** — so the atomic RPC has never existed in production and **every send this product has ever made fell through to a read-then-write fallback**: two concurrent sends both read N and both write N+1, losing a count. Fixed three ways rather than one: ① the migration is in the runner; ② **the fallback no longer increments — it RECOMPUTES from `figsy_sent_emails`**, which is race-free by construction and structurally enforces the function's own stated invariant that the counter *"can never exceed the send log"*, and both its error paths are now checked (#349) with a message saying **the email WAS sent** so nobody re-sends and double-mails a prospect; ③ **the System page can now detect a missing FUNCTION at all** — it could ask about tables and columns and had no way to ask about functions, which is precisely why this hid for a month. Red-proved: the old strategy genuinely returns **1 when 2 emails were sent**, and `classifyProbeError(err,'function')` did not exist on the old file — it returns `unknowable`, never `missing`, for an RLS refusal, a timeout, a paused project or **a missing TABLE inside the function** (that last one matters: reporting it as a missing function sends someone to re-create something already there). **#644 FIXED — a client can now reach their replies.** `/milla/replies` did not exist as a route; it does now, rendering the REAL inbox rather than a second copy that would drift. The rail gains a **Replies** entry badged with the live count, and the recent-reply rows became `<Link>`s instead of inert `<div>`s. Guarded by `replies-reachable.test.ts`, which asserts **reachability rather than rendering** — the gap that let this survive 2,157 tests, every one of which asked whether something renders and none whether it can be reached. Red-proved: 4 of its 6 assertions fail against the pre-fix shell. **#376 #383 #473 REVERTED 🟢 → 🩷**, and the script had to grow a door to allow it: `flip-dots.sh` refuses to un-green by design, so **`FOUNDER_UNFLIP=1` was added — refused by default, same shape as `FOUNDER_FLIP`** — because *reverting a wrongly-awarded green must be as deliberate and auditable as awarding one.* Each row is chained with why. **Two greens were then AWARDED on evidence that does exist**: **#563** (the money screen knows the pack — proven in BOTH states, exhausted and brand-new, the second being the exact reader that row was written about) and **#625** (the fail-closed gate fired with the wallet held at $4,000 — and the Integrity panel in the same screenshot still shows the 2 older leads *"charged for work that never started"*, so charge-then-fail sits beside refuse-then-don't-charge in one frame). **#625's re-approve path was explicitly NOT claimed** — the walk approved a fresh lead, not a second time. **Also fixed:** the cockpit's **`R16.19/$`** — a rand rate on a UK founder's daily dashboard, a South-African leftover; the same FX response already carried GBP and it was being ignored. The ZAR conversion machinery is **untouched on purpose** (`amount_zar` holds real historic rows; rewriting unverified revenue maths would trade a cosmetic defect for a money one) — the revenue page still headlining *"MRR (ZAR)"* is logged as #647 rather than silently changed. And the **"awaiting" label collision** that cost the walk a step: the System page now says *"awaiting the CLIENT's approval"*, the Lead queue says *"No drafts awaiting YOUR approval"* — named for **who decides**. **THE WALK ARTIFACT'S BATCH BUTTONS WERE THE ROOT CAUSE AND ALL TEN ARE FIXED**, not just PROBE's: every one now reads *"These steps passed — flip the N they cover"* and carries the warning that it flips only what its steps exercise; the J5 card no longer invites flipping 11 items on A18's evidence when A18 does not prove #66, #472 or #185. The header states the lesson plainly, because the mechanism that produced three false greens will produce three more otherwise.
- **12 Aug (🚶 THE FIRST A11 WALK — 5 items turned 🟢 on the founder's own eyes, and pressing ONE button found a client-facing dead end 2,157 tests never could):** the founder walked J5 + J9 + PROBE guided one step at a time. **THE FINDING: a client cannot reach their own replies.** Milla's rail names the reply and nothing opens it — `MillaShell.tsx:176` renders it as a plain `<div>` with **no link, no click handler, no route**, and the rail has no Replies/Inbox entry, so there is no other path; the reply screen exists and works. **The reply is the thing the client pays for** (#644, 🔴, critical band). The gate reads as a sales minimum; it is very nearly the break-even line. The row's 6-Aug *"the count in this row may be wrong"* mailbox question is **closed by R14** — "4 across 2" is correct and is the whole scope. ✅ **AND THE INTERMITTENT GATE FAILURE IS IDENTIFIED AND FIXED — it was guarding P12.** Fable saw `1 failed | 2156 passed` twice on 11 Aug, could not name it (the dot reporter scrolled) and shipped after it passed on a re-run. It reproduced here and the log named it: **`website-freeze.test.ts` — "Test timed out in 5000ms", a TIMEOUT, not an assertion.** Measured: the suite hashes **110 files / 68.1 MB** and takes **~1,240 ms on an IDLE container** against vitest's **5,000 ms** default — a ~4× margin that `check.sh` erases by running two `next build`s and 124 test files alongside it. **The severity is the point: this is the guard on the founder-locked website (#605, P12).** A randomly-failing guard trains everyone to re-run until green — which is precisely what happened today — so the next red might be a real unapproved website change and get shrugged at. Fixed with an explicit **60 s** budget (~48× idle), which cannot mask a real failure because genuine drift asserts immediately with a diff. **Proved both directions without touching a single website file:** manifest and disk agree on all 110 (green), and a tampered hash in a COPY of the manifest is still detected (red). ⚠️ **JOB 1 (bulk-close the 72 dead audit issues) is BLOCKED AT 1 OF 72 — GitHub's hourly API limit was already exhausted when the run started.** All 72 were listed and verified first: **every one matches `🔴 KIND Audit — …`, zero exceptions, nothing outside the pattern.** A comment posted on **#919** before the wall; its close failed, so **#919 currently carries a "closing as not planned" comment while still open** — the next run must close it first. ⚠️ **And listing them surfaced a real trap: the GitHub issue-number space and the PRODUCT-INVENTORY id space COLLIDE.** GitHub issue **#637** is one of the dead audit issues; inventory **#637** is the audit *repair* item. And one detour worth recording: his password contained `@`, the URI delimiter between password and host, so it was **reset to alphanumeric** rather than percent-encoded — a mis-encoded character fails with a misleading DNS/tenant error, not a password one.)* **WHAT THIS ACTUALLY TURNED OFF, none of it new code:** #343 the cron single-run guard (two replicas doubling every email and every charge — the reason A15 existed) · **#342, whose lapse cron had thrown 500 every night, silently, since the day it shipped** · #340, where a declined card was written to our database as `active` · **#366, where a repeat client's second month sourced ZERO new people** because `pdl_scroll_token` did not exist and every run re-read page one · #554b (`client_inboxes` had no RLS at all) and #350's `USING(true)` policies · #627's `app_settings`, which made **A17** a two-minute job minutes later (*"Sourcing is now capped at $100 a month"* — the same card that threw a schema-cache error 24 hours earlier). **AND #554 ANSWERED FOR THE FIRST TIME EVER:** *"No exposed tables. 39 deny-all · 43 scoped · 82 tables read"*, read off production rather than inferred from files. Two causes: the entry gave no column so the probe asked for `id`, which `cron_claims` (composite PK `(job, slot)`) does not have; and `schema()` had ONE failure branch that called **every** error MISSING — a missing column, a timeout, an auth failure and a blank response all rendering as "run this migration". **That is `schema-probe.ts`'s own header law inverted** — *"a probe that could not run returns UNKNOWABLE, never 'missing'"* — the #565 class on the one screen built to end it, and it cost an hour of re-running a migration that had already succeeded. I wrote a wrong interpretation without re-reading the locked decision that was two rows away, it survived review, merged, and the only check that fired was the founder's memory. The same pass also left a formatting bug — the #549 note landed in the OWNER column of the inventory table, which doc-lint does not inspect. **Both corrected as corrections** (inventory row + LAUNCH-PAD ruling band; PR #1292's merged body stands as the record of the error). **The standing rule this mints: before writing ANY sentence about a founder-locked decision, re-read the lock itself — the ⚖️/founder-locked text — and cite it. A lock that is not re-read before being paraphrased is a lock that gets paraphrased wrong.** One genuinely open edge surfaced by the correction, flagged not assumed: "unlimited warmup on Growth" is our own 26-Jul research note, not a vendor confirmation — one founder glance before client #1 settles it.
- **6 Aug (🧰 A20 — THE AUDIT REPAIR BATCH, and the guard caught four bugs in ITSELF before it caught anything else):** the ten defects the founder's gut called for are fixed. `20260806_audit_columns` (both homes, AR6) creates `figsy_sent_emails.client_id`/`.status` and four `clients` columns and **backfills client_id from campaign_id**; both send inserts now write it, including the **day-1 path — which needs it most, because it carries `campaign_id: null` so no backfill can ever reach it, and it is the first email any prospect receives.** **FOUR OF THE FINDINGS WERE NOT MISSING COLUMNS BUT WRONG NAMES, and creating them would have frozen the mistake (O8):** `figsy_enrollments.emails_sent` is `current_step` (its own schema comment: *"0 = not started, 1 = step 1 sent"*); `figsy_campaigns.step1_subject` was **deleted, because the A/B winner check never read it** — dead weight that rejected the whole query, so that check has never examined a single campaign; `reply_count`/`enrolled_count` are `replies_total`/`leads_enrolled`, so every morning brief had been reporting a confident 0% reply rate derived from a rejected query; `icps.locations` is `geographies`, and `icps.description` does not exist at all — suggest-campaign has been prompting Claude with **no ICP context**, producing generic suggestions that look personalised. `figsy_sessions` is not a table: `status.ts` now counts distinct clients in `figsy_chat_messages`. **THE GUARD IS THE DELIVERABLE (#642):** `schema-truth.ts` + 28 tests sweep every table, every button against all 408 routes, every `.rpc()` against every `CREATE FUNCTION`. **It failed on itself four times first, and every failure is now a named test** — a router declared plainly as `router` was invisible to the pattern (11 live routes reported dead), prose inside a write read as columns (`'New ICP: fintech founders'` → a column `icp`), a ternary else-branch read as a key (`isDemo ? null : x` → a column `null`), and its own explanatory comment read as a dead button. **Two scanners are excluded by name and the exclusion is ASSERTED, not assumed** — a test proves neither touches a database, because a scanner's docs necessarily quote example queries (`schema-drift.ts` once reported a table called `table` from its own comment). **And one limit is stated rather than papered over:** the button guard cannot catch #640, whose double prefix only exists once a wrapper is applied to its argument — that has its own regression pin instead. Red proofs taken against `git show` copies: 6 pre-fix column violations and the `admin/clients/${id}/usage` offender, both caught. `lena.ts` deleted (registered, never mounted, zero callers). #643 probe extended with all five audit columns so one press proves production converged. Undeclared-write pin **4 → 3** — `clients` left by being fixed, the only good reason it ever falls. 2,134 tests, gate 6/6. **Flips: #637 #638 #639 #640 #641 #642 #643.**

the guard generalizes to every table + a dead-button test (#642); the schema probe extends so one press proves production (#643). **All 13 money RPCs clean.** ⚠️ For `clients.*` columns the repo proves only the repo never created them — the hand-paste era may have; every fix is `IF NOT EXISTS`, correct in both worlds. Board: Σ602 · A18 struck · A20 (audit repair) added to the critical band. Report delivered table-view; Opus prompt issued; nothing built yet — report first, then the word.
- **6 Aug (🔢 R3/R11 — THE WEBSITE WAS NEVER THE BUG: FOUR STEP LIMITS WERE LIVE AT ONCE):** the founder challenged the fix scope — *"4 gaps found is highly untrustworthy… audit not by memory"* — and he was right twice over. The copy claim was in **six** places, not four, and the copy was the SYMPTOM. **`sequence-quality.ts` blocked activation at 7 · `operator.ts` let an operator SAVE 10 · the Vida editor and the CLIENT's own editor each hard-coded a bare 7 · and the client-facing Sequences page, linked in BOTH portal sidebars, capped at 3 and told the client "(max 3)"** — while five website pages and the landing page promised 10. **A prospect could read 10, a client be shown 3, and the system enforce 7.** Every one of those was individually plausible; the defect existed only BETWEEN them. **Cured by giving the number one home** — `MAX_SEQUENCE_STEPS` in `@kind/shared`, which all four apps can read (it lived in `apps/api`, which `apps/admin` cannot import from, and that gap is precisely why the copies bred). ⚠️ **A comment in the Vida editor cited `sequence-cap-agreement.test.ts` as "the only thing making the copy safe" — THAT FILE HAS NEVER EXISTED**: a safety net asserted in a comment and never built. **And the old cap test went RED for the correct fix**, because it pinned the literal `steps.length < 7` rather than the agreement — O8's lesson arriving in person, rewritten to assert the constant. `website-step-claims.test.ts` now binds every step-count claim across all four apps to that constant; **the website-freeze guard fired exactly as designed** on the four changed pages and was re-baselined under R3, which is the sanctioned path, not a silencing. **R11 (docs current) cost more honesty than work:** my "66 + 30 false claims" were PATTERN HITS, not verified claims — most were correct history, and three of my first automated edits CORRUPTED facts (Apollo's own $99 plan price inside a legal doc, a runbook's deliberate "not $99" contrast, and three historical bug records). **Reverted and done by hand.** The genuinely-false live claims were fewer but far worse: **`sales-playbook.md` — the document the founder SELLS from — carried a header labelled "PRICING (locked)" still quoting the retired $1-reveal/$3-work ladder**, plus an objection-handling script promising *"free to start… just $1 per lead revealed"*, and `client-flow-sop.md` still sent clients to a "Start free trial" button retired on 1 Aug. `doc-lint` now bans those exact patterns, **and its DOCS list was widened — `sales-playbook.md` was never in it, which is why a selling document rotted for two weeks while six maintained docs stayed clean.** A `⚠️ HISTORICAL` banner at the top of a file is now load-bearing and announced, never a silent skip. 2,143 tests, gate 6/6.
- **6 Aug (🗳️ ELEVEN RULINGS IN ONE PASS — register §12, and the founder's own screenshot settled the one number nobody had checked):** the founder went down the standings report line by line and ruled everything open. **R1** free-10 is a sales tool, built only when a real client needs it — the no-freebies lock stands. **R2** stealth NARROWED not lifted: company page yes, no personal announcement, warm outreach only — *"stealth within a trust circle"*. **R3** the site's *"up to 10 steps"* is COMMANDED FIXED across four live pages, one of them inside a POPIA paragraph in the Terms, against a product that hard-stops at 7 — and **R4** overrides RULEBOOK §11 for that change ONLY, recorded as an explicit exception because a silent one is how a rule dies. **R5** Kevin is uncertain, not rejected — *"not sure about friends and business"* — while the post-launch GitHub fix stays essential and independent of him. **R6** IDs #632–#636 reserved and logged as reserved, not minted. **R7** A9 and A19 re-dated to *"when funds allow"*: both need real money to move, and neither may ever be reported as slipping. **R8 is the big one — 🩷 IS NOW A CRITICAL STATE.** 281 items are live that no human has walked, A11 moves into the critical band, and **all 281 get walked in ranked sessions, money and send path first**: *"if they pink and never walked to green before a client comes its unsure it holds… customer facing we screwed."* He is right, and the evidence is this very day — #599, #637–#641 and A18's own blocker were every one of them a 🩷 that turned out broken. **R9** the M&V site hierarchy is killed outright rather than parked. **R10** #426 is superseded out of the 🔴 count (it asked for a 10-step cap; the answer was 7, and 7 is enforced) — 🔴 172, ⏸ 6. **R11** all docs current NOW, not after launch. **AND R12/R13 FROM HIS OWN SCREENSHOT of Instantly's comparison table:** *"unlimited email warmup"* is **vendor-confirmed on Growth** — our note was right, the ladder maths holds, and the same table shows API/webhooks are HyperGrowth-only, which is **independent vendor proof of his 30-Jul #577 amendment** and of why #549 does not matter. - **6 Aug (🔒 THE REPORT LOCK + THE DEPLOY DIAGNOSIS):** the founder locked the standings-report format — his words: *"i cant trust the docs at the moment. and i cant trust this source. so give me a way to work"* → **register §11, RPT1–RPT4**: one shape forever, every table ends Owner · When, two board lines (main vs awaiting his merge), no item silently dropped between reports, and a **saved prompt he pastes that binds all of it** — every number verified live, nothing from memory. And the recurring `Failed to create code snapshot` deploy failures were MEASURED, not guessed: each `railway up` uploads the ~137MB tracked tree once per service — four times per ship — and Railway's ingest times out on the payload. `docs/` is 25MB of it and no deploy has ever needed it: **`.railwayignore` now cuts ~100MB per ship** (docs/ + .github/, deliberately nothing a build reads). - **6 Aug (🩹 #599 — THE CSV IMPORTER HAS NEVER WORKED, AND A DOC PREDICTED IT IN WRITING A WEEK AGO):** A18 needed one test lead planted, so the operator CSV import was run on a real file **for the first time since it shipped on 30 Jul** and returned `Could not find the 'source' column of 'leads' in the schema cache` — **0 of 1 rows.** `toLeadRow` has written `source: 'csv_import'` since the day it was written and **no migration ever created the column.** The item sat at 🩷 *live* for a week; it is now 🟡, which is the only honest dot for something nobody had ever run. **`docs/SCHEMA-DRIFT.md` called this exact shot on 30 Jul** — *"if the column does not exist, the CSV import fails on the first real Apollo file"* — and it was filed as a ❓ unknowable rather than fixed, which is the lesson: **a correctly-written prediction is not a mitigation.** **The expensive half was silent.** The second writer, `lib/vida.ts`, destructured `{ data: row }` with no error check, so the rejected insert returned `leadId = null` and **every inbound website-chat visitor who typed in their email failed to become a lead — for weeks, with nothing logged.** Those are hand-raisers. Grepping every other `leads` query found two more queries naming columns that do not exist, neither of which any test had ever touched: `/coaching` rendered **every booked meeting as "Prospect"** with no title, company or score, and `/coaching/:leadId/brief` answered **404 "Lead not found" for every lead that exists** — the prep brief has never once been generated. Both selected `why_fits`, which is not a column at all but the name of a RESPONSE field built by scrubbing `score_reasoning` (#492/F2). **And the hunt turned up a SHADOWED DUPLICATE ROUTE:** a second `figsyRouter.get('/activity')` ~2,000 lines below the live one, which Express has never reached — **I edited it for ten minutes believing I was fixing production, and every check stayed green.** Deleted. **FIXED:** `20260806_leads_source` creates the column (nullable, no default — historic provenance is genuinely unknown, and a DEFAULT would stamp every old row with a claim nobody checked); the `vida.ts` insert is error-checked; the `why_fits` selects are gone; the live activity feed reports a failed read instead of rendering it as a quiet week. **THE REAL DELIVERABLE IS THE GUARD, because none of this was subtle and 2,105 tests missed all of it — nothing compared a query's column names to the schema.** `leads-column-truth.test.ts` now does, generically, for every column `toLeadRow` writes and every column any `leads` query selects; `no-duplicate-routes.test.ts` fails the build on a path registered twice. **One vacuous assertion caught in the act:** my first version of the error-check guard matched `/\.error\b/`, which passes on `console.error` in an unrelated catch block — proved vacuous by running it against the pre-fix file, then replaced with a real behaviour test. Red proofs taken against `git show` copies rather than `git checkout`, per the rule that has now cost work twice. **Flips: #599 (🩷 → 🟡, corrected) · #631 (🟡 → 🩷).**

- **6 Aug (🔎 THE '0 RUNS EVER' DOCTRINE DIES + THREE FOUNDER RULINGS):** the founder opened his own Actions tab and it showed **788 workflow runs** against docs — CLAUDE.md included — swearing CI had never run once. Verified: **Actions ran 25 May → 3 Jul** (last run: Tests #148 on PR #922, merged 3 Jul 07:27), then the account flag killed it dead — ~370 PRs have merged into silence since. **The 27 Jul check that minted the doctrine was BLIND:** the workflow-runs API endpoint returns 0 through this tooling while the founder's screen shows 788 — the #565 class (a broken check rendering as a clean answer), landed in the constitution itself and repeated to the founder as fact the same morning. - **6 Aug (🧰 #627 · #628 — THE REPAIR BATCH: a table that never existed, a page that oversold and undersold at once, and the launch pad's runlist dug out from under 200 lines of dead prose):** every finding came from the founder walking his own product to demo it — the third session running where the walk beat the test suite. **#627 — `app_settings` DID NOT EXIST.** He pressed Save on the #626 cap card and got *"Could not find the table 'public.app_settings' in the schema cache"*. Not in the repo, not in production, never made by anybody. **The System check had read it for months** and reported *"no usable setting exists — set it"*, because a missing TABLE and a missing ROW shared one sentence — and the fixes are opposite: run a migration versus type a number. **The check that should have caught it is exactly why nobody did.** It surfaced only because the WRITE is checked (#349): reads had been swallowing it since the probe was built. Migration written to **both real homes**, `isMissingTable()` hoisted so a third copy cannot appear, and both surfaces now name which problem they have. ⚠️ **And my own 5-Aug SCHEMA-DRIFT entry was false** — it claimed the table *"exists in production because somebody made it there"*, inferred from "the probe reads it and nothing errors", which was never evidence. Corrected **as a correction, quoted not deleted**; the undeclared-write pin drops 6→5 for the only good reason it ever can, which is that the table is now declared. **#628 — the Settings page was overselling and underselling in the same scroll.** It advertised a white-label product that does not exist with **"30% recurring"** under a **"Scale plan"** chip, on a live client surface, against a partner ledger that pays **25%** — a prospect could have quoted that number back at us. - **5 Aug (🕳️ #625 — TWO GUARDS, AND THE FAILURE WALKED BETWEEN THEM):** the founder's A9 money walk approved two leads on Client Zero and nothing enrolled. `approveLead`'s no-campaign gate at 3c fails closed so nobody pays for work that cannot run — but the **#424 charge-once branch at 3b returns before it ever runs**, and the `.catch` alert wrapped around `autoEnrollLead` never fired because its no-campaign branch is a **silent return, not a throw**. Revealed, enrolled nowhere, reported *"approved"*, surfaced only by the Integrity panel hours later. **The fix was not to copy the lookup:** 3c also RESUMES a paused campaign (the cold-suspension comeback), so a copy would have rescued a returning client at one door and locked them out at the other — both doors now share one resolver and one refusal. The silent return also learned to speak, but **only on `force`**: a human approval bought that work, while the cron path stays quiet because a campaignless client is ordinary there and alerting would train the founder to ignore the alert that matters. **The walk keeps earning its keep** — this is the third real defect it has produced (#619 the board lies, #623 the calculated "$ in", now #625), none of which 1,981 passing tests had questioned. Also raised from the same screens: the **`cron_claims` migration is MISSING in production** (two replicas would double every email and every charge — a founder click in Vida → Engine), Instantly holds **9 mailboxes across 3 domains** where every plan assumed 4 on 2, and `pdl_monthly_cap_usd` is unset.
- **5 Aug (📮 #624 — THE REPLY-PATH ROW WAS GREEN AND HALF-BLIND, and the half it skipped loses every reply):** the System check verified the inbound signing secret and counted replies — and **never asked where replies are addressed.** Outreach is sent by SMTP from the warmed Google mailbox, but every email carries a `Reply-To` from `COLD_REPLY_TO` (`FIGSY_COLD_REPLY_TO` → `FIGSY_REPLY_TO` → a **silent hardcoded** `hello@get-kind.com`), and replies arrive only via Resend's inbound webhook. So the screen could read green while every reply went somewhere nobody wired up — **a campaign with no return path**, failing silently, because an empty Unibox looks exactly like a quiet market. Found chasing the founder's Gmail question; it matters because he runs send-day **alone**. Now one pure `replyPathVerdict` puts the resolved address, its source, the Resend domain verdict and the last-reply age on screen. **It refuses to overclaim:** Resend lists SENDING domains, receiving also needs MX, so a listed domain is necessary-not-sufficient and the row says so; unreachable Resend is NOT-MEASURED, never OK. And 0 replies means opposite things either side of send-day — expected before, **the signature of a dead return path** after. Closes the last 🤖 item on the critical list.
- **5 Aug (💷 #623 — THE FOUNDER'S "SIMPLE MATHS" FOUND A MONEY LIE THE TESTS COULD NOT):** walking A9 he approved two leads and asked why Vida did not show **$8 more earned**. It did not, and it was right not to — both contacts were already paid for under **#424 charge-once**, so no money moved. But chasing *why* exposed that the board's *"$ in"* was **arithmetic on the approval count** (`$299 + (approved − 100) × $4`), not a reading of the ledger — so on any client with a repeat contact it prints cash that never arrived. **#619's failure, pointing the other way:** that one was a comped account claiming a payment, this one a real client's board inflating what they paid us. Now `moneyInUsd()` sums the ledger — referenced purchases only, with `manual_grant`, `usage $0`, `wallet_charge` and unreferenced rows all excluded by name and for stated reasons. **⚠️ It also surfaced the NEXT overstatement, unasked:** refunds are `type: 'refund'` with a negative amount **outside** `PURCHASE_TX_TYPES`, and the query never fetched them — so a fully refunded client would have kept reading *"$299 in"*. A referenced refund now subtracts and the query widened; an over-refund is left **negative rather than clamped**, because hiding that anomaly would be the same bug a third time. The money engine itself was never wrong and was not touched. **The lesson is the walk:** 1,938 passing tests never questioned the figure, because none of them asked what the number was supposed to MEAN. The founder did.
- **5 Aug (🏛️ B1 DONE — ICO REGISTRATION, the legal floor):** founder registered with the ICO (~£47/yr Tier 1, direct debit).
